| [v](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Conservatism_in_Colombia)[t](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:Conservatism_in_Colombia?action=edit\&redlink=1)[e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:Conservatism_in_Colombia) |

The [Colombian Conservative Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Conservative_Party), founded in 1849, traces its origins to opponents of General [Francisco de Paula Santander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Francisco_de_Paula_Santander)'s 1833–1837 administration. While the term "liberal" had been used to describe all political forces in Colombia, the conservatives began describing themselves as "conservative liberals" and their opponents as "red liberals". From the 1860s until the present, the party has supported strong central government and the Catholic Church, especially its role as protector of the sanctity of the family, and opposed [separation of church and state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_church_and_state). Its policies include the legal equality of all men, the citizen's right to own property, and opposition to dictatorship. It has usually been Colombia's second largest party, with the [Colombian Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombian_Liberal_Party) being the largest.[^cite_note-348]

### North America

Main article: [Conservatism in North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_North_America)

North American conservatism, combining [traditionalist conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Traditionalist_conservatism), [economic liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_liberalism), and [right-wing populism](8354382.md), is different from European conservatism and can be traced back to the [classical liberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_liberalism) of the 18th and 19th centuries,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEAdams200132-349] although Canada developed an American-style conservatism that competed with the older [Tory conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toryism).[^cite_note-350] According to political scientist [Louis Hartz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Louis_Hartz), French Canada is a fragment of feudal Europe, whereas the United States and English Canada are liberal fragments.[^cite_note-351] Sociologist [Reginald Bibby](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reginald_Bibby) asserts that conservatism has been strong and enduring throughout North America because of the propagation of religious values from generation to generation.[^cite_note-352]

#### Canada

Main article: [Conservatism in Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservatism_in_Canada)

Canada's conservatives had their roots in the Tory [loyalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loyalism) who left America after the [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution).[^cite_note-353] They developed in the socio-economic and political cleavages that existed during the first three decades of the 19th century and had the support of the mercantile, professional, and religious elites in Ontario and to a lesser extent in Quebec. Holding a monopoly over administrative and judicial offices, they were called the [Family Compact](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_Compact) in Ontario and the *[Chateau Clique](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chateau_Clique)* in Quebec. [John A. Macdonald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_A._Macdonald)'s successful leadership of the movement to confederate the provinces, and his subsequent tenure as prime minister for most of the late 19th century, rested on his ability to bring together the English-speaking Protestant aristocracy and the [ultramontane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultramontane) Catholic hierarchy of Quebec and to keep them united in a conservative coalition.[^cite_note-354]

The conservatives combined [Toryism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toryism) and pro-market liberalism. They generally supported an activist government and state intervention in the marketplace, and their policies were marked by *[noblesse oblige](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Noblesse_oblige)*—a paternalistic responsibility of the elites for the less well-off.[^cite_note-355] The party was known as the Progressive Conservatives from 1942 until 2003, when the party merged with the [Canadian Alliance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canadian_Alliance) to form the [Conservative Party of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_of_Canada).[^cite_note-356]

The conservative and [autonomist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomism_in_Quebec) [Union Nationale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Nationale_\(Quebec\)), led by [Maurice Duplessis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maurice_Duplessis), governed the province of Quebec in periods from 1936 to 1960 and in a close alliance with the Catholic Church, small rural elites, farmers, and business elites. This period, known by liberals as the [Great Darkness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grande_Noirceur), ended with the [Quiet Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quiet_Revolution) and the party went into terminal decline.[^cite_note-357]

By the end of the 1960s, the political debate in Quebec centered around the question of independence, opposing the [social democratic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_democracy) and [sovereignist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_sovereignty_movement) [Parti Québécois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parti_Qu%C3%A9b%C3%A9cois) and the [centrist](39619035.md) and [federalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federalism_in_Quebec) [Quebec Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Liberal_Party), therefore marginalizing the conservative movement. Most French Canadian conservatives rallied either the [Quebec Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quebec_Liberal_Party) or the [Parti Québécois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parti_Qu%C3%A9b%C3%A9cois), while some of them still tried to offer an autonomist third-way with what was left of the [Union Nationale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Nationale_\(Quebec\)) or the more populists [Ralliement créditiste du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ralliement_cr%C3%A9ditiste_du_Qu%C3%A9bec) and [Parti national populaire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parti_national_populaire), but by the [1981 provincial election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1981_Quebec_general_election) politically organized conservatism had been obliterated in Quebec. It slowly started to revive at the [1994 provincial election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Quebec_general_election) with the [Action démocratique du Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Action_d%C3%A9mocratique_du_Qu%C3%A9bec), who served as [Official opposition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_opposition) in the [National Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Quebec) from 2007 to 2008, before merging in 2012 with [François Legault](6899647.md)'s [Coalition Avenir Québec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coalition_Avenir_Qu%C3%A9bec), which took power in 2018. The modern [Conservative Party of Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Party_of_Canada) has rebranded conservatism and, under the leadership of [Stephen Harper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Harper), added more conservative policies.

[Yoram Hazony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yoram_Hazony), a scholar on the history and ideology of conservatism, identified Canadian psychologist [Jordan Peterson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jordan_Peterson) as the most significant conservative thinker to appear in the English-speaking world in a generation.[^cite_note-358]

The meaning of conservatism in the United States is different from the way the word is used elsewhere. Following the [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution), Americans rejected the core ideals of European conservatism, which were based on landed nobility, hereditary monarchy, established churches, and powerful armies. However, the prominent American conservative historian [Russell Kirk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russell_Kirk) argued, in his influential work *[The Conservative Mind](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Conservative_Mind)* (1953), that conservatism had been brought to the United States and he interpreted the [American Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Revolution) as a "conservative revolution" against royal innovation.[^cite_note-359] The revolution was also supported by Anglo-Irish statesman [Edmund Burke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edmund_Burke), widely known as the father of conservatism, although Burke and a few [Founding Fathers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers), most notably [John Adams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Adams), were highly critical of the [French Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_Revolution).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTENeill202147-360]

American conservatism is a broad system of political beliefs in the [United States](3434750.md), which is characterized by respect for American traditions, support for [Judeo-Christian values](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judeo-Christian_ethics), economic liberalism, anti-communism, and a defense of [Western culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_culture). [Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty) within the bounds of conformity to conservatism is a core value, with a particular emphasis on strengthening the [free market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_market), limiting the size and scope of government, and opposing high taxes as well as government or labor union encroachment on the entrepreneur.

The 1830s [Democratic Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Democratic_Party_\(United_States\)) became divided between [Southern Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Democrats), who supported slavery, secession, and later segregation, and the [Northern Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Democrats), who tended to support the abolition of slavery, union, and equality.[^cite_note-361] Many Democrats were conservative in the sense that they wanted things to be like they were in the past, especially as far as race was concerned. They generally favored poorer farmers and urban workers, and were hostile to banks, industrialization, and high tariffs.[^cite_note-362]

The post-Civil War [Republican Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Republican_Party_\(United_States\)) had conservative factions, but was not uniformly conservative. The Southern Democrats united with pro-segregation Northern Republicans to form the [Conservative Coalition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Coalition), which successfully put an end to Blacks being elected to national political office until 1967, when [Edward Brooke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Brooke) was elected Senator from Massachusetts.[^cite_note-363][^cite_note-364] [Conservative Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Democrat) influenced US politics until 1994's [Republican Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Republican_Revolution), as the American South [shifted from solid Democrat to solid Republican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_strategy), while maintaining its conservative values.

In late 19th century, the [Democratic Party](5043544.md) split into two factions; the more conservative Eastern business faction (led by [Grover Cleveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grover_Cleveland)) favored gold, while the South and West (led by [William Jennings Bryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Jennings_Bryan)) wanted more silver in order to raise prices for their crops. In 1892, Cleveland won the election on a conservative platform, which supported maintaining the gold standard, reducing tariffs, and taking a *laissez-faire* approach to government intervention. A severe nationwide [depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panic_of_1893) ruined his plans. Many of his supporters in 1896 supported the [Gold Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Democratic_Party_\(United_States\)) when liberal [William Jennings Bryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Jennings_Bryan) won the nomination and campaigned for [bimetallism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bimetallism), money backed by both gold and silver. The conservative wing nominated [Alton B. Parker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alton_B._Parker) in 1904, but he got very few votes.[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366]

The major conservative party in the United States today is the Republican Party, also known as the GOP (Grand Old Party). Modern American conservatives often consider [individual liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individualism) as the fundamental trait of democracy, as long as it conforms to conservative values, [small government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_government), [deregulation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deregulation) of the government, and economic liberalism—which contrasts with [modern American liberals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Liberalism_in_the_United_States), who generally place a greater value on [social equality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_equality) and [social justice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_justice).[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368] Other major priorities within American conservatism include support for the nuclear family, law and order, the [right to bear arms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_to_bear_arms), [Christian values](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_values), anti-communism, and a defense of "Western civilization from the challenges of modernist culture and totalitarian governments".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchneider2009xii-369] Economic conservatives and libertarians favor small government, low taxes, limited regulation, and free enterprise. Some social conservatives see traditional social values threatened by secularism; so, they support [school prayer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/School_prayer), and [oppose abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition_to_the_legalization_of_abortion).[^cite_note-Social-370] [Neoconservatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoconservatives) want to expand American ideals throughout the world, and show a strong support for Israel.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFrohnen2006ix–xiv-371] [Paleoconservatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleoconservatives) oppose [multiculturalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism) and press for restrictions on immigration.[^cite_note-Paleoconservative2-372]

The conservative movement of the 1950s attempted to bring together the divergent conservative strands, stressing the need for unity to prevent the spread of "godless communism", which Reagan later labeled an "[evil empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evil_Empire_speech)".[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374] During the [Reagan administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan_administration), conservatives also supported the so-called [Reagan Doctrine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan_Doctrine), under which the US as part of a Cold War strategy provided military and other support to guerrilla insurgencies that were fighting governments identified as socialist or communist. The Reagan administration also adopted [neoliberalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoliberalism) and [Reaganomics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reaganomics) (pejoratively referred to as [trickle-down economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trickle-down_economics)), resulting in the 1980s economic growth and trillion-dollar deficits. Other modern conservative positions include [anti-environmentalism](8149492.md).[^cite_note-375] On average, American conservatives desire tougher foreign policies than liberals do.[^cite_note-376]

The [Tea Party movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_Party_movement), founded in 2009, proved a large outlet for populist American conservative ideas. Their stated goals included rigorous adherence to the US constitution, lower taxes, and opposition to a growing role for the federal government in health care. Electorally, it was considered a key force in Republicans reclaiming control of the US House of Representatives in 2010.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378]

Long-term shifts in conservative thinking following the election of [Donald Trump](4848272.md) have been described as a "new fusionism" of traditional conservative ideology and right-wing populist themes.[^cite_note-Fusionism-379] These have resulted in shifts towards greater support for [national conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_conservatism),[^cite_note-380] [protectionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protectionism),[^cite_note-381] [cultural conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cultural_conservatism), a more [realist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realism_\(international_relations\)) foreign policy, a repudiation of [neoconservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoconservatism), reduced efforts to roll back entitlement programs, and a disdain for traditional checks and balances.[^cite_note-Fusionism-379]

The [Liberal Party of Australia](18453.md) adheres to the principles of [social conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_conservatism) and [liberal conservatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberal_conservatism).[^cite_note-382] It is liberal in the sense of economics. Commentators explain: "In America, 'liberal' means left-of-center, and it is a pejorative term when used by conservatives in adversarial political debate. In Australia, of course, the conservatives are in the Liberal Party."[^cite_note-383] The [National Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Right_\(Liberal_Party_of_Australia\)) is the most organized and reactionary of the three factions within the party.[^cite_note-384]

Political scientist [James Jupp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Jupp) writes that "\[the] decline in English influences on Australian reformism and radicalism, and appropriation of the symbols of Empire by conservatives continued under the Liberal Party leadership of Sir [Robert Menzies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Menzies), which lasted until 1966".[^cite_note-385]

Other conservative parties are the [National Party of Australia](21927.md) (a sister party of the Liberals), [Family First Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_First_Party), [Democratic Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_Labour_Party_\(Australia,_1980\)), [Shooters, Fishers and Farmers Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shooters,_Fishers_and_Farmers_Party), [Australian Conservatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Conservatives), and the [Katter's Australian Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katter's_Australian_Party).

The largest party in the country is the [Australian Labor Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Australian_Labor_Party), and its dominant faction is [Labor Right](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Labor_Right), a [socially conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socially_conservative) element. Australia undertook significant economic reform under the Labor Party in the mid-1980s. Consequently, issues like protectionism, welfare reform, privatization, and deregulation are no longer debated in the political space as they are in Europe or North America.

[Historic conservatism in New Zealand](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_conservatism_in_New_Zealand) traces its roots to the unorganized conservative opposition to the [New Zealand Liberal Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Liberal_Party) in the late 19th century. In 1909 this ideological strand found a more organized expression in the [Reform Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Party_\(New_Zealand\)), a forerunner to the contemporary [New Zealand National Party](333149.md), which absorbed historic conservative elements.[^cite_note-386] The National Party, established in 1936, embodies a spectrum of tendencies, including conservative and liberal. Throughout its history, the party has oscillated between periods of conservative emphasis and liberal reform. Its stated values include "individual freedom and choice" and "limited government".[^cite_note-387]

In the 1980s and 1990s both the National Party and its main opposing party, the traditionally left-wing [Labour Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_Labour_Party), implemented free-market reforms.[^cite_note-388]

The [New Zealand First](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Zealand_First) party, which split from the National Party in 1993, espouses nationalist and conservative principles.[^cite_note-389]

## Psychology

See also: [Biology and political orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biology_and_political_orientation)

### Conscientiousness

[The Big Five personality model](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Five_Personality_Traits) has applications in the study of [political psychology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Political_psychology). It has been found by several studies that individuals who score high in [Conscientiousness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conscientiousness) (the quality of working hard and being careful) are more likely to possess a right-wing political identification.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391][^cite_note-392] Since conscientiousness is positively related to job performance,[^cite_note-393][^cite_note-394] a 2021 study found that conservative service workers earn higher ratings, evaluations, and tips than social liberal ones.[^cite_note-395]

### Disgust sensitivity

A number of studies have found that [disgust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disgust) is tightly linked to political orientation. People who are highly sensitive to disgusting images are more likely to align with the political right and value traditional ideals of bodily and spiritual purity, tending to oppose, for example, [abortion](765.md) and [gay marriage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gay_marriage).[^cite_note-396][^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399]

Research in the field of [evolutionary psychology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evolutionary_psychology) has also found that people who are more disgust sensitive tend to favor their own [in-group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-group) over [out-groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outgroup_\(sociology\)).[^cite_note-400] A proposed reason for this phenomenon is that people begin to associate outsiders with disease while associating health with people similar to themselves.[^cite_note-401]

The higher one's disgust sensitivity is, the greater the tendency to make more conservative moral judgments. Disgust sensitivity is associated with moral [hypervigilance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypervigilance), which means that people who have higher disgust sensitivity are more likely to think that suspects of a crime are guilty. They also tend to view them as evil, if found guilty, and endorse harsher punishment in the setting of a court.[^cite_note-402]

### Authoritarianism

[Right-wing authoritarianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-wing_authoritarianism) (RWA) is a set of attitudes that describe somebody who is highly submissive to their authority figures, acts aggressively in the name of said authorities, and is conformist in thought and behavior.[^cite_note-APA_Dictionary-403] According to psychologist [Bob Altemeyer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Altemeyer), individuals who are politically conservative tend to score high on RWA.[^cite_note-404]

A study done on Israeli and Palestinian students in Israel found that RWA scores of right-wing party supporters were significantly higher than those of left-wing party supporters.[^cite_note-405] However, a 2005 study by psychologist H. Michael Crowson and colleagues suggested a moderate gap between RWA and other conservative positions, stating that their "results indicated that conservatism is not synonymous with RWA".[^cite_note-406]

According to political scientist [Karen Stenner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Stenner), who specializes in authoritarianism, conservatives will embrace diversity and civil liberties to the extent that they are institutionalized traditions in the social order, but they tend to be drawn to authoritarianism when public opinion is fractious and there is a loss of confidence in public institutions.[^cite_note-407]

### Ambiguity intolerance

In 1973, psychologist [Glenn Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Glenn_Wilson_\(psychologist\)) published an influential book providing evidence that a general factor underlying conservative beliefs is "fear of uncertainty".[^cite_note-408] A meta-analysis of research literature found that many factors, such as [intolerance of ambiguity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ambiguity_tolerance) and need for [cognitive closure](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cognitive_closure_\(psychology\)), contribute to the degree of one's political conservatism and its manifestations in decision-making.[^cite_note-Jost_et_al._2003-409][^cite_note-410] A study by Kathleen Maclay stated that these traits "might be associated with such generally valued characteristics as personal commitment and unwavering loyalty". The research also suggested that while most people are resistant to change, social liberals are more tolerant of it.[^cite_note-411]

### Social dominance orientation

[Social dominance orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_dominance_orientation) (SDO) is a [personality trait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Personality_trait) measuring an individual's support for [social hierarchy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_hierarchy) and the extent to which they desire their [in-group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-group) be superior to [out-groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/In-group_and_out-group). Psychologist [Felicia Pratto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felicia_Pratto) and her colleagues have found evidence to support the claim that a high SDO is strongly correlated with conservative views and opposition to [social engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_engineering_\(political_science\)) to promote equality. Pratto and her colleagues also found that high SDO scores were highly correlated with measures of [prejudice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prejudice).[^cite_note-412]

However, psychologist [David J. Schneider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_J._Schneider) argued for a more complex relationships between the three factors, writing that "correlations between prejudice and political conservatism are reduced virtually to zero when controls for SDO are instituted, suggesting that the conservatism–prejudice link is caused by SDO".[^cite_note-413] [British conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_conservative) political theorist [Kenneth Minogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenneth_Minogue) criticized Pratto's work, saying:

> It is characteristic of the conservative temperament to value established identities, to praise habit and to respect prejudice, not because it is irrational, but because such things anchor the darting impulses of human beings in solidities of custom which we do not often begin to value until we are already losing them. Radicalism often generates youth movements, while conservatism is a condition found among the mature, who have discovered what it is in life they most value.[^cite_note-414]

A 1996 study by Pratto and her colleagues examined the topic of [racism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racism). Contrary to what these theorists predicted, correlations between conservatism and racism were strongest among the most educated individuals, and weakest among the least educated. They also found that the correlation between racism and conservatism could be accounted for by their mutual relationship with SDO.[^cite_note-sidanius96-415]

### Happiness

In his book *Gross National Happiness* (2008), [Arthur C. Brooks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_C._Brooks) presents the finding that conservatives are roughly twice as happy as social liberals.[^cite_note-416] A 2008 study suggested that conservatives tend to be happier than social liberals because of their tendency to justify the current state of affairs and to remain unbothered by inequalities in society.[^cite_note-napier_and_jost_2008-417] A 2012 study disputed this hypothesis, demonstrating that conservatives expressed greater personal agency (e.g., personal control, responsibility), more positive outlook (e.g., optimism, self-worth), and more transcendent moral beliefs (e.g., greater religiosity, greater moral clarity).[^cite_note-418]
